REGIONAL MUNICIPALITY OF WATERLOO 
Budget Highlights 
2026 Operating Budget 
 Average Property Tax Budget Impact is 5.1% 
 Total operating budget expenditure of $1,801 million ($1,593 million 
net of transfers to Capital and Reserve Funds) 
 Assessment Growth is 1.5% 
Changes to the Region's user fees and charges include: 
 Water Supply increase of 5.9% 
 Wastewater Treatment increase of 5.9% 
 Water Distribution increase of 3.9% 
 Wastewater Collection increase of 4.9% 
 User fees and charges for various programs including Transit, Waste 
Management, Transportation, Airport Services, Seniors’ Services, 
Cultural Services, Region of Waterloo Library, Public Health Programs 
and Paramedic Services.
 
2026-2035 Capital Budget and Forecast 
 The 2026 approved capital budget is $772 million 
 The 10 year capital program is $8.1 billion (constant 2026 dollars) 
 Total debenture requirement over the 10 year program is $0.9 billion 
Property Tax and User Rate Debenture requirement is $471 million 
Development Charge debenture requirement is $390 million

2025 2026 $ Change % Change 
Water $230.05 $243.63 $13.58 5.90% 
Wastewater $284.81 $301.61 $16.80 5.90%

2025 2026 $ Change % Change 
Retail Water $782.82 $808.30 $25.48 3.25%

Note: Approved rate increases effective January 1, 2026 for wholesale water supply, wholesale wastewater 
treatment, retail water distribution, and retail wastewater collection are 5.9%, 5.9%, 3.9% and 4.9% 
respectively. For 2026, the monthly service charge has remained consistent from 2025 at $12/month for 
retail water distribution and $11/month for retail wastewater collection. Retail water and wastewater rates 
apply to the Townships of Wellesley and North Dumfries and include wholesale water supply and 
wastewater treatment costs as noted above. 
* Based on an average residential property valued at $354,500, based on a valuation date of January 1, 
2016, and water usage of 182 cubic metres

Notes: 
- 2026-2035 capital programs are stated in 2026 dollars. 
- Projects in the capital program which are classified under the unsecured funding category may not proceed in the absence of identified funding sources, such as additional provincial or federal 
government subsidies. 
- The passing of Bill 17 (Protect Ontario by Building Faster and Smarter Act, 2025) will impact the funding of future growth-related capital works. Certain provisions from the Bill are in effect while 
others will not be reflected until the next Regional Development charge Background Study is completed and a new by-law is in place. As such, the development charge funding assumptions reflect the 
pre-Bill 17 rules. The full impact of the Bill will be reflected in future capital plans.

Region of Waterloo
2026 Program Budgets
19
        
    
  
        
     
    
  
   
      
   
       
         
     
Community 
Services 
Community Services supports residents through all stages of life as we build a resilient, 
inclusive community. We operate programs and services that help people experiencing 
homelessness, people living on limited incomes, newcomers, families, and older adults. 
Our teams coordinate local systems so people can access the right help at the right 
time in their lives. Services include community housing, a municipal long-term care 
home, a home child care program, child care fee subsidy program, and refugee 
resettlement coordination. We also plan and deliver system-wide programs, such as 
implementing the Canada-Wide Early Learning and Child Care Plan, act as the Service 
System Manager for homelessness and licensed child care, deliver Ontario Works, and 
deliver systems planning for newcomers to Waterloo Region. 
Our Service Philosophy is “People First. Relationships Matter.” Some services in 
Community Services are either cost-shared or fully funded by the province of Ontario, and 
some are fully funded by the Region.

The following services are included 
in the 2026 Plan and Budget: 
Children’s Services: 
Implementing the Canada-wide Early Learning and Child Care (CWELCC) 
programs, including a directed growth plan. 
Implementing the Early Years and Child Care system plan, and an access and 
inclusion plan. 
Funding CWELCC child care operators, special needs resourcing services and 
EarlyON Child and Family Centres through contractual service agreements. 
Supporting quality professional learning and capacity building of early years 
and child care programs. 
Funding and administering the Child Care Subsidy program. 
Operating a licensed Home Child Care program. 
Employment and Income Support: 
Delivering the Ontario Works program. 
Operating as an Employment Ontario provider for the Ministry of Labour, Immigration, 
Training & Skills Development. 
Supporting people seeking employment through job fairs, workshops, resume 
help and interview preparation. 
Overseeing the innovative Pathway to Employment Program, helping people 
find work and gain training in the construction industry. 
Providing financial support to help people living with low incomes to pay utility 
bills. 
Operating three “Welcome Space” locations in Cambridge, Kitchener and 
Waterloo with in-person service navigation for clients of Community Services 
programs and services.

Housing Services: 
Homelessness prevention, outreach and The Plan to End Chronic Homelessness. 
Creating and preserving affordable housing, including the Building Better Futures 
Plan. 
Operating Waterloo Region Housing's community housing units. 
Overseeing local co-op and non-profit housing units. 
Transitional housing and emergency shelters. 
Partnering with housing providers to retain existing community housing. Supporting 
families with the Equity-based Rent Assistance Program. 
Immigration Partnership: 
Leading the Immigration Partnership Council and Steering Groups. 
Planning, implementing and reporting for Waterloo Region Immigration Strategy, 
which includes multi-sector service coordination and enhancement, and supports 
employers to leverage immigrant talent. 
Overseeing large-scale refugee resettlement coordination. 
Leading research, advocacy and intergovernmental relations for immigration initiatives. 
Seniors’ Services: 
Operating Sunnyside Home, our long-term care centre. 
Implementation The Butterfly Approach® to support the emotional needs of 
people living with dementia in Sunnyside Home. 
Overseeing provincially-funded programs that support seniors who need support 
to live on their own. 
Offering a variety of programs and services to support the needs of people living 
with dementia. 
Operating Sunnyside Supportive Housing, an affordable housing building for low-
income seniors living with mental health and frailty. 
Operating a homemaking support program to support people living in the community to 
maintain their housing and independence. 
Operating a Seniors’ Wellness Clinic under a partnership model to deliver care, such as 
physiotherapy and community paramedicine. 
Strategy, Performance and Partnerships: 
Leading data and analytics across teams to inform decisions. 
Strengthening shared systems across Community Services through system 
partnerships, strategic planning, and quality assurance. 
Coordination of social planning, policy and research.

Region of Waterloo
2026 Program Budgets
36
 
         
        
 
         
           
  
         
      
         
       
          
   
Transportation 
Services 
Transportation Services works to help move people and goods safely and efficiently 
across Waterloo Region. We provide critical connections for this community, including travel 
options for residents who don’t have a car. 
TransportationServices isresponsible for public transit servicesthat operate acrossthe 
cities and townships.Theseservices includeION lightrail, express buses, local buses, and 
flextransit. 
This department is also responsible for ensuring the safe design and operation of all 
Regional roads. This includes infrastructure such as traffic signals, roundabouts, 
sidewalks, street lighting, pedestrianislands, pavement marking, traffic signals and road 
signs. We keep local roads working safely through pothole repairs, grass cutting, flooding 
and erosion control, pavement markings, and more. Our teams also work hard to help keep 
roads safe through winter salting and snow plowing. 

===== PAGE 37 =====
       
 
 
    
 
 
  
 
         
 
        
 
      
   
      
      
 
       
 
  
 
      
   
          
 
      
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Transportation Services: 
Operating and maintaining Regional roads (asphalt, grass, landscaping, 
drainage, winter maintenance). 
Designing, operating, and maintaining traffic signals, roundabouts, and 
controlled intersections. 
Regional transportation network planning. Active 
transportation planning. 
Transportation 10-year capital program planning. 
Completing mandatory regional infrastructure inspections and 
condition reporting. 
Optimizing traffic flow with Miovision technology. 
Transit Services: 
Regional bus and LRT service. 
Specialized transportation. 
Integrating transit and shared micromobility program with the active transportation 
network. 
Customer service, marketing and communications.

Engineering and 
Environmental 
Services 
The Region’s Engineering and Environmental Services Department provides 
essential services that Region ofWaterloo residentsrelyonevery day.Wecare for each 
other and the public as we deliver reliable, essential environmental services and 
infrastructure projects to connect our growing rural and urban communities. 
We build and improve our roads, bridges, active transportation and transit facilities, 
water and wastewaterfacilities, underground services and waste management 
facilities. 
We also manage and maintain the Region’s buildings and vehicle fleet, ensuring 
we’re prepared to support residents with our services. We oversee the Region’s 
assets and plan for future growth. Maintaining this infrastructure is essential to 
supporting daily life for every person in our Region and expanding it is key to 
supporting our growing population. 
Our team ensures there is clean, reliable drinking water across the community by 
managing Waterloo Region’s unique water supply system and wastewater 
treatment plants. We also manage the community's two waste management sites, 
including a landfill, and provide waste collection and diversion programs. 

===== PAGE 58 =====

♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Design and Construction: 
Building the infrastructure that supports every resident and visitor in 
Waterloo Region on behalf of our internal clients in Water and Wastewater, 
Waste, Transportation, and Transit). 
Delivering over $300 million of capital program work on behalf of our internal 
clients annually. Around 50 active construction projects and over 100 
projects in the design stage each year. 
Facilities and Fleet Management: 
Responsible for keeping more than 7 million square feet of Regional 
building assets operational and maintained. 
Planning, designing, and constructing over 300 projects annually for 
other Regional divisions (Housing and Homelessness, PSV, Transit, 
WRPS, Water, etc.). 
Supporting and maintaining 800 regional vehicles and pieces of 
equipment. 
Strategic Initiatives & Asset Management: 
Developing policies and delivering on strategic initiatives whichdrive 
operational and project efficiencies. 
Managing the Corporate Asset Management Program and developing a 
Project Management Office.

Waste Management: 
Managing residential waste collection and diversion programs, which 
includes processing about 200,000 metric tonnes of garbage, yard waste 
and green bin organics per year. 
Managing the community’s landfill and transfer station, downtown 
business collection and public drop off services. 
Monitoring and operating former and current waste management sites, 
complying with environmental legislation and regulations. 
Water and Wastewater Infrastructure Management: 
Planning for growth through the Water Supply Strategy and Wastewater 
Treatment Long Term Strategy. 
Inspecting infrastructure and developing the renewal and growth capital 
plan. 
Protecting water supply through groundwater protection initiatives. 
Reviewing approximately 400 development applications annually. 
Water and Wastewater Operations: 
Supplying 125 million litres of drinking water per day for residents and businesses. 
Operating and maintaining 50 water treatment facilities, 100+ wells, 15 
pumping stations and 17 tanks and reservoirs in the Region. 
Overseeing 13 wastewater treatment plants that protect the environment 
by treating 155 million litres of wastewater per day. 
Protecting the environment through effective sanitary sewer enforcement, spills 
response and industrial wastewater monitoring.

Region of Waterloo
2026 Program Budgets
99
 
Planning,
Development and 
Legislative Services 
The Planning, Development and Legislative Services (PDLS) Department helps shape a sustainable, 
prosperous, and connected Waterloo Region. Our teams work together to plan for growth, steward 
the Region’s natural and cultural assets, and provide essential corporate and community services 
that support residents every day. 
We ensure that growth and development are well-coordinated and integrated with Regional 
infrastructure and service delivery, supporting complete and thriving communities across Waterloo 
Region. We operate the Region of Waterloo International Airport, a key driver of economic 
development, innovation, and tourism. 
PDLS strengthens community connection and belonging by supporting arts, culture, heritage, and 
learning. We operate the Region of Waterloo Library and museums, manage and install public art, 
and fund local cultural initiatives that showcase the spirit and creativity of our region. 
We protect the environment and lead the Region’s transition to a low-carbon future by implementing 
corporate climate change initiatives and supporting community led climate strategies. We also 
steward 16 Regional forests that contribute to the Region’s biodiversity, climate resilience, and 
overall quality of life. 
Our department also delivers vital corporate and legislative services that keep the Region running 
smoothly, from legal counsel, real estate, licensing, and by-law enforcement to information 
management, archives, and the Provincial Offences Court. 
Together, PDLS provides the foundation that supports growth, culture, sustainability, and good 
governance helping to build a resilient and thriving Waterloo Region for generations to come.

The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Build Waterloo Region: 
Land development for affordable housing, job creation and important 
community infrastructure. 
Aerospace and Aviation cluster support and land development at YKF. 
Provides technical support and analysis for future industry investment. 
Supporting children & youth wellness and belonging through service provider 
system coordination. 
ROW International Airport: 
Operating, maintaining and growing airport service 24/7/365. 
Constructing and developing airport infrastructure. 
Fostering the aviation and aerospace sector by working with airport 
tenants and partners (Waterloo Wellington Flight Centre, Waterloo 
Institute of Sustainable Aviation, etc.). 
Regional Growth, Development and Sustainability Services: 
Reviewing development applications for matters of Regional interests 
such as water, wastewater, paramedic services, public health and transit. 
Developing climate mitigation and adaption strategic plans, policies and 
programs. 
Ongoing stewardship of Regional Forests. 
Data and planning services related to Regional growth. 
Reviewing and issuing Regional Road access permits.

Council and Administrative Services: 
Supporting Regional Council and Standing Committee meetings 
Coordinating elections for Regional Chair and directly-elected Councillors. 
Supporting the Integrity Commissioner and Code of Conduct 
Administering the Lobbyist Registry. 
Providing information management and privacy support and services for the 
organization. 
Licensing and Enforcement Services. 
Provincial Offenses: 
Operating the Provincial Offences Court. 
Provincial Offences Prosecution service. 
Legal Services: 
Civil Litigation and Administrative Tribunals. 
Real estate services including valuation, negotiation, acquisition, 
disposition, leasing and expropriations. 
Corporate legal advice and services to Region departments, including on municipal 
authority and jurisdiction. 
Cultural Services: 
Administering and maintaining the Region’s public art collection. 
Archaeological work to support REDI goals and legislative responsibilities. 
Stewarding Region of Waterloo Museums: collections and design, coordination and 
marketing of exhibits and community programming. Operating the organization’s 
Corporate Archives. 
Administering multiple arts and culture grants and incentiveprograms 
(includes: Key Cultural Institutions, Major Arts and Culture Organizations, 
Cultural Drivers of Tourism and Region of Waterloo Arts Funding). 
Operating the collections, events and programming for the Region of 
Waterloo Library system’s headquarters and 11 branches.

123
 
  
Public Health and 
Paramedic Services 
The Public Health and Paramedic Services Department provides programs and services 
that meet critical health needs and keep Waterloo Region residents healthy. These 
services are either cost-shared or fully funded and mandated by the province of Ontario. 
Public Health teams focus on promoting and protecting health while preventing disease. 
Our services include health inspections, infectious disease control and prevention 
measures, vaccinations, dental and sexual health services, and neighborhood health 
programs. Our work also encompass prevention-based initiatives such as parenting and 
child health services. 
Paramedic Services deliver life-saving emergency care, hospital transport, and upstream 
Community Paramedicine services across Waterloo Region. Our team is dedicated to 
ensuring that patients receive the right response from the right resource, at the right time. 
The goals include achieving the fastest possible response times for those who are 
acutely ill, along with providing care that embodies compassion, dignity, and 
professionalism.

The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Health Protection and Healthy Environments: 
Conducting inspections to ensure food safety standards are met, and addressing 
violations to protect community health. 
Monitoring and responding efficiently to disease outbreaks to minimize impact 
and maintain community safety. 
Ensuring compliance with infection control standards in various facilities 
to prevent disease transmission. 
Investigating potential health hazards and implementing measures to 
mitigate risks. 
Inspecting drinking and recreational water sources to ensure safety and 
prevent contamination. 
Investigating and controlling the spread of rabies and vector-borne 
diseases. 
Infectious Diseases: 
Implementing comprehensive strategies to prevent and manage the 
spread of infectious diseases within the community. 
Protecting our residents from vaccine-preventable diseases by: 
Administering vaccines to Grade 7 students to protect against 
common diseases. 
Ensuring compliance with the provincial Immunization of School Pupils 
Act requirements. 
Offering vaccines to protect against seasonal respiratory illnesses 
Coordinating the distribution and management of vaccines to health care 
providers. 
Providing services and education to promote sexual health, including: 
Counselling and clinics to support youth and underserved populations. 
Supporting treatment and prevention of sexually transmitted and 
blood-borne infections.

Child, Family and Dental Health: 
Providing support and resources to families for the healthy development of children with 
the Healthy Babies, Healthy Children program. 
Delivering prevention and dental care access through school screenings. and 
accessible dental clinics to improve oral health for children and youth. 
Offering dental care services for low-income adults and seniors to promote oral health 
and enhance quality of life. 
Implementing educational programs aimed at preventing substance use among youth, 
fostering well-being and healthier lifestyle choices. 
Offering prenatal support and education to promote the health and well- being 
of expectant parents and their babies. 
Providing guidance and resources for new parents, including support for baby feeding. 
Strategy, Performance and Partnerships: 
Overseeing provincial organizational standards and effective public health practice. 
Analyzing health trends and data to understand the needs of the community 
and improve health outcomes. 
Advancing health equity by reducing barriers so all community members have 
access to health resources and opportunities. 
Collaborating with local organizations and agencies in system partnerships to 
support positive community changes. 
Safeguarding community health through expert guidance of the medical team. 
Preparing for public health emergencies with response plans to protect the community. 
Paramedic Services: 
Providing 911 emergency response and ambulance transport. 
Initiating in-home care and connecting residents to health resources through 
the Community Paramedicine Program. 
Ensuring compliance with the Ambulance Act and associated Standards 
through quality assurance, professional practice, staff training. 
Reducing Ambulance Offload Times in partnership with local hospitals and the 
province with the Dedicated Offload Nurse Program. 
Supporting the community through coordination of the Public Access 
Defibrillation program.

136
 
 
 
Office of the Chief 
Administrative 
Officer 
The Office of the Chief Administrative Officer provides overall leadership and direction for 
all eight Region of Waterloo departments. We work to foster a culture that reflects the 
Region's values, ensure that our services are delivered efficiently and effectively, and 
advance organization-wide conversations, policy changes and actions. 
The CAO’s office also includes the Region’s Office of Reconciliation, Equity, Diversity and 
Inclusion (REDI) and Accessibility. We work on the structural, institutional changes and 
investments needed to address historic and ongoing systemic oppression. Through this 
work, the Region is committed to creating a community where everyone feels safe and 
included. We can do this by shifting culture, having courageous conversations, ensuring 
everyone is heard and seen, inviting community to co-create, and working towards Truth and 
Reconciliation. 

===== PAGE 137 =====
       
    
 
      
         
 
 
          
 
     
 
         
 
 
          
       
         
 
         
 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Office of the Chief Administrative Officer 
Contributing to the culture of the organization and exemplifying leadership 
behaviours aligned with the Region’s Mission, Vision and Core Value. 
Supporting the development of the leadership team on key collective 
skillsets such as business case analysis and leadership and decision making 
under uncertainty. 
Reconciliation, Equity, Diversity and Inclusion 
Establishing and sustaining trusting relationships with the community 
through the Community Safety and Wellbeing Plan, the Reconciliation Action 
Partnership (RAP), and the organizational Community Engagement 
Community of Practice. 
Investing in community by administering Upstream Fund grants Investing in 
community through the Reconciliation Equity Investment Fund. 
Implementing the REDI organizational plan to help embed REDI into 
everything we do. 
Leading and supporting the Region to make our services accessible, 
including AODA compliance and reporting.

142
 
 
Resident Experience,
Strategy and 
Communications 
The Department of Resident Experience, Strategy and Communications (RESC) supports 
residents by developing strategic plans tailored to community needs and providing round-
the- clock access to services. 
The team collaborates with Regional Council and all departments to create and align 
strategic action plans, ensuring effective delivery of services, programs, and infrastructure. 
The team reports quarterly on performance to Council, staff, and community. Engagement 
with the community and other government levels is key, as RESC actively advocates for 
critical funding and policy changes. 
The team prioritizes community voices by creating engagement opportunities, conducting 
surveys, analyzing data, and designing services with an equity-focused, human-centered 
lens. 
RESC also provides clear public information and 24/7 multi-channel customer service via the 
Service First Contact Centre, in-person locations, the Region’s website, and social media 
platforms.

The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Communications and Engagement: 
Strategic Communications: Leading and supporting effective strategic and 
project communications and engagement plans. 
Proactive Media Relations: Crafting engaging news releases and leading media 
relations to ensure resident access to information. 
Interactive Social Media Management: Fostering vibrant digital community connections. 
Creative Multi Media Services: Offering innovative graphic design, 
compelling video production, and printing solutions. 
Responsive Customer Service: Providing round-the-clock support via phone, live 
chat, and email through the Service First Contact Centre. 
Welcoming Reception Services: Ensuring a friendly and efficient reception experience. 
Comprehensive Resident Support: Enhancing access through websites, EngageWR, 
and informative newsletters. 
Effective Employee Communication: Cultivating engagement within the 
workforce and improved access to staff information. 
Corporate Strategy, Performance, and Resident Experience: 
Strategic Planning & Implementation: Driving corporate strategies with, through 
community engagement, data analytics and foresight. 
Aligned Annual Business Planning: Synchronizing resources and budgets with 
strategic priorities for optimized outcomes. 
Performance Excellence: Fostering organizational continuous improvement, 
measurement, and transparent reporting. 
Innovative Service Design: Leading UX and research initiatives with a user-
centered approach to support improved in-person and digital self serve options. 
Cultural Transformation: Supporting organizational culture change for 
enhanced effectiveness. 
Inclusive Interpreter and Translation Services: Ensuring accessibility and understanding 
across languages for residents. 
Commissionerof Resident Experience, Strategy & Communications: 
Strategic Intergovernmental Relations: Building powerful advocacy and 
partnerships with government entities. 
Effective Issues Management: Navigating challenges to ensure appropriate strategic 
action and resolution. 
Council Relations Excellence: Providing strategic support for effective 
Council partnerships.

149
 
Corporate 
Services 
Corporate Services provides the financial, technological, and risk management 
foundations that enable every Regional program to operate effectively. The department also 
serves as a strategic advisor to Council, the CAO, and all departments, offering expert 
guidance on long-term financial sustainability, technology, legislative impacts, major 
capital planning, and emerging risks. 
Through this work, Corporate Services ensures the Region remains financially resilient, 
digitally secure, and equipped to meet the community’s needs both today and over the 
long term. 

===== PAGE 150 =====

The following services are included 
in the 2026 Plan and Budget: 
Information Technology Services 
Protecting all Regional information assets, systems, and networks 
Managing and integrating corporate applications to streamline operations 
Governing and maintaining enterprise data and analytics capabilities 
Supporting staff through end-user devices, infrastructure, and IT service management 
Planning and delivering major IT strategies, modernization initiatives, and digital service 
improvements 
Corporate Finance 
Leading enterprise financial planning and analysis 
Managing the Regional Budget (valued at $2.3 billion in 2025) and long-term 
financial forecasts 
Overseeing development charges, reserve funds, and growth-related financial strategies 
Providing financial reporting, accounting oversight, and policy development 
Advising departments and Council on financial implications for major projects, 
legislation, and strategic priorities 
Treasury Services 
Delivering banking, investment management, cash flow control, billing, collections, and 
accounts payable 
Overseeing debt financing, credit rating interactions, and preparation of financial statements 
Providing internal audit and statutory financial reporting to strengthen accountability 
Managing corporate inventory operations and distribution of materials across Regional 
programs 
Leading fair, open, and transparent procurement processes and ensuring compliance with 
legislation and policy 
Insurance & Risk Management 
Managing the Region’s insurance portfolio, claims, and loss recovery 
Participating in the Waterloo Region Municipalities Insurance Pool to ensure cost-
effective coverage and shared risk strategies 
Providing enterprise risk assessment, mitigation strategies, and contract risk advice 
Supporting operational departments in identifying and managing risk exposures

Region of Waterloo
2026 Program Budgets
158
 
Human 
Resources 
The Human Resources department provides leadership and support to the entire 
organization by supporting our people, developing a strong, professional workforce and 
fostering service excellence. Engaged and supported employees are key to achieve our 
strategic goals. HR does behind-the-scenes work that is critical for the organization’s 
success, including employee recruitment, retention, training and engagement, as well as 
compensation management, benefit and system administration, health and safety and 
labour relations. 
Human Resources also includes the emergency management team, which prepares for 
emergency events in Waterloo Region and responds when the community is in crisis. 

===== PAGE 159 =====
       
    
 
    
      
 
           
 
        
 
     
 
 
 
 
       
 
 
        
 
  
 
 
      
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
♦ 
The following services are 
included in the 2026 Plan and 
Budget: 
Commissioner of Human Resources: 
Responsible for overall departmental management, including fiscal 
oversight, leadership development, and strategic direction. 
Reviewing and updating policies and practices to ensures alignment with legislation, 
organizational goals, and best practices. 
Emergency Management Office to lead preparedness activities, response, and 
business continuity work. 
Development and HR People Partners: 
Organization Development delivers programs to enhance performance, 
develop capabilities, and foster a positive employee experience at the 
Region. 
Human Resources People Partners work alongside business leaders to align 
HR initiatives with organizational goals, driving success by fostering talent 
development, enhancing the employee experience and optimizing workforce 
performance. 
Learning and Development empowers the organization by facilitating 
continuous skill enhancement and knowledge acquisition, enabling employees 
to learn, grow, innovate, and change to become future ready. 
Employee Relations: 
Support positive labour relations through collective bargaining, contract. 
administration, and conflict resolution. 
Talent acquisition through equitable recruitment plans. 
Region of Waterloo 2026 Program Budgets 159

♦ 
♦

Wellbeing and Occupational Health and Safety: 
Fleet and Occupational Health and Safety - manages the corporation’s 
Health & Safety Program, and fleet safety programs as per legislation, 
policies, and procedures. 
Abilities Management –manages disability management, return to work, 
employee health services, and WSIB, STD and LTD claims management. 
Promoting employee health and wellbeing - corporate employee health and 
wellness initiatives consistent with the principles of the National Standard for 
Psychological Health and Safety, implementation, and evaluation of corporate 
wellness initiatives. 
Developing a respectful workplace – builds a culture of greater inclusivity and 
respectful behaviour by managing a safe space for employees to raise workplace 
issues, and to minimize and resolve conflict early and proactively. 
Total Rewards: 
Compensation and Benefits – offers a holistic package of compensation, 
benefits, and OMERS pension to attract and retain talent. 
HR systems - efficiently manages all employee data and core HR processes using 
HR Systems and supports critical decision making. 
Payroll- manages payroll and financial aspects of OMERS pension 
administration for the Region per policies, collective agreements, and 
statutory regulations.
